### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%， 顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域 。 數據公布後 ， 市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫 。 聯準會理事鮑曼表示 ， 決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果 ； 舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示 ， 不宜對升息過於保守 ， 並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示 ， 在能源價格波動下 ，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決， 歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Oct/19/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.890，低點在 32.520，以 32.338 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 4 角 6.3 分。
上週台幣走貶 ， 周初 ， 外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢 ， 雖逢高吸引出口商拋匯 ，
仍不敵外資匯出力道令台幣走貶 ； 週二台股重挫近千點 ， 外資大幅賣超台股1653 億
並匯出，台幣接連跌破 32.000、32.100 及 32.200，總成交量 71.3 億，創歷史新高；
周三台股收漲，外資雙向進出，台幣收斂部分跌幅；週四，受美國通膨高於預期影
響，早盤台幣跳空開低，台股跌逾2%，外資大幅賣超 3025 億，台幣跌破 31.400 關
卡 ， 總成交量91.2 億 ， 再創歷史新高 ； 週五外資持續匯出 ， 台幣貶至周內低點32.520，
終場在央行調節之下收在 32.338。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 CPI、PPI 雙雙高於預期，通

膨壓力仍在，儘管聯準會官員陸續發言表示不急於升息，淡化市場對於聯準會十月
升息預期，惟年底前聯準會仍有升息一碼空間，利率持穩於高位將給予國際美元支
撐；就台灣市場來看，市場對AI 產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，若
賣壓延續，將壓抑市場風險偏好，增加外資調整台股部位及匯出的壓力，令台幣面
臨持續貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯將抑制台幣貶值速度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣
走勢偏弱。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.100-32.700

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/19/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。上週人民幣中間價連續走升，週三中間價升至6.7249 創
三年八個月新高，惟市場靜待美國公布通膨數據，交投謹慎，人民幣僅小幅走升；
週三晚間，美國公布 9 月 CPI 年增率 3.7%、核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，雙雙高於預期，
帶動美元指數升至年內高點 102.945、 人民幣貶至周內低點6.7005； 然多位聯準會官
員發言表示聯準會不急於升息，令市場對於聯準會十月升息預期略有降溫，美元指
數自高點回落，激勵人民幣由貶轉升；臨近周末，聯準會主席華許於IMF 會議發言
並未指引十月聯準會政策利率走向，再度淡化市場對於聯準會十月升息預期，國際
美元走勢偏弱，人民幣走升觸及年內高點 6.6825，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6850，週
線升值 91 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6870，週線升值 60 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。就國際市場來看，美國仍面臨高通膨、年底可能將
再次升息，利差因素支撐美元指數於高檔震盪，限縮人民幣升值空間；就中國國內
市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人
民幣表現；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，匯價維持在五日均線之下交
投 ， 均線呈現空頭排列 ， 加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.6900 整數關卡 ， 顯示USD/CNH
空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6500-6.7000

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，受油價攀升推升美國通膨與利率預期帶動，美
元指數溫和走升創 6 月底以來新高，推升 USD/JPY 突破 158.50；週間，美國公布 9
月 CPI 高於預期，帶動美元指數升至 2025 年 5 月以來高點，也使 USD/JPY 最高來
到 158.94，然逢高獲利了結賣壓出籠使匯價回落；臨近週末，USD/JPY 一度下探至
157.53，隨後逢低買盤進場收復部分跌幅，終場收在 157.99，週線下跌 13 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受美伊戰事推升汽柴油飆漲
與服務成本擴散影響，美國 9 月 CPI 及 PPI 月增漲幅均超出市場預期，加上初請失
業金人數連五週低於 20 萬人顯示勞動市場穩定，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月再次升
息，美日實質利差維持高檔，持續為USD/JPY 提供下檔支撐。二、被視為鴿派的日
本經濟財政大臣城內實明確表態，貨幣政策應交由日本央行自行判斷，並宣告日本
已擺脫長期通縮、轉型為增長導向經濟體。此番言論降低政治干預日銀進一步升息
的疑慮，在貨幣政策正常化的腳步下，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。三、就技
術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價目前交投於月線及年線之間，各天期均線高度
糾結，多空雙方於 158 關卡附近拉鋸，在有效突破季線及年線反壓或跌破月線支撐
前 ， 仍以區間震盪看待。 綜合以上 ， 預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高 。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日圓：157.00-159.00 
前週交易區間 
157.53 158.94

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
歐元兌美元匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，持續受法國財政赤字與歐債利差擔憂拖累，
EUR/USD 震盪走弱；週間，因美國9 月 CPI 高於預期，市場重新評估聯準會年底前
升息腳步，導致 EUR/USD 跳水最低來到 1.1112；臨近週末，儘管美國 PPI 與零售
銷售數據偏熱，市場優先反映美債殖利率回落與風險資產重新定價，美元多頭獲利
了結帶動 EUR/USD 谷底反彈 ， 最高來到1.1269， 終場收在1.1239， 週線上漲42 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、法國央行月度展望報
告指出，法國第三季 GDP 預估僅微幅成長 0.1%，印證近期高能源的成本已實質拖
累歐元區核心成員國增長。同時，歐洲央行總裁拉加德明確拒絕出手干預法債，要
求法國透過自身財政政策化解赤字困境；在明年法國大選前的財政隱憂與基本面疲
弱下，預計將令歐元上檔持續承壓。二、儘管歐洲央行管委溫施與總裁拉加德保留
12 月升息預期，加上德國基建與軍費支出為歐元提供部分下檔保護，但市場穩固預
期聯準會年底前將再升息一次。在美國經濟韌性與高利差優勢主導下，預計將限縮
歐元反彈空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 自低點反彈並站回五日線
之上，然而中長期均線下彎仍維持空頭排列，RSI 指標亦處於 50 中軸下方，顯示整
體空方趨勢尚未扭轉，反彈至月線附近恐面臨較大賣壓。綜合以上，預估EUR/USD
本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1300 
前週交易區間 
1.1112 1.1269

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115. 10.12-115.10.16 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.020~2.025% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連續減發定存單使市場資金寬鬆 ， 雖外資大舉匯
出，資金供給仍充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.58%， 低於市場
預期區間 1.60%-1.62%，較上期標售結果下跌 5.8 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.88%，後隨美債利率滑落，加上本週
2 年期公債標債結果提振市場持券信心，帶動 10 年期指標券 A15109 週四向
下成交在 2.02%~2.025%。 
在銀行去化短期資金及補充 LCR 之需求下 ， 週三2 年期公債得標利率為1.65%，
落在市場預估區間下緣 ， 投標倍數回升至2.86 倍 ， 銀行業得標比重上升至88%。
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率大幅回檔，促使台債交投偏空氛圍轉趨和
緩 ， 惟在央行維持緊縮貨幣政策下 ， 買盤積極追價的意願不強 ， 預期短線台債
殖利率呈區間格局的可能性較高，關注下週 10 年券標售結果，預估 5 年券區
間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.98%~2.03%。

市場焦點
美國
科技股
• 美國企業獲利維持強勁 ，背後主要受經濟展望穩健及 AI資本支出熱潮支撐 。從獲利來看 ，市
場共識預期標普500企業EPS成長率2026年達28%，2027年及2028年則分別為20%及16%，
雖呈現逐步降溫 ，但仍維持雙位數增長 。標普500預期本益比已由約 23倍降至19倍，且並非
主要來自股價修正 ，而是企業獲利預期上調所致 ，在企業獲利維持成長 、AI投資持續挹注及
估值已較高點收斂的情況下，標普500仍具中長期配置價值。
美債
外匯對沖視角
• 在全球公債遭遇拋售潮與通膨陰影下 ，美債因利率期間結構與匯率優勢 ，今年以美元計價總
回報優於多數G7主權債，儘管海外私人機構投資人正在等待長端利率落底訊號以重返市場 ，
對美元資金或以美元計價為主的投資人而言 ，當前美債名目殖利率仍處於數十年高位 ，具備

相當良好的長期鎖利價值，但價格波動風險考量下，建議債券佈局仍以中短天期為主。
台股
• 8月景氣對策燈號續亮紅燈，綜合判斷分數41分，較前月持平，對應的燈號仍是代表景氣熱絡
的紅燈，連續第9個月亮出紅燈，追平史上最長「連紅」紀錄，顯示國內經濟維持成長。
• 根據歷史統計第四季台股上漲的機率接近八成 ，除了AI投資趨勢未減 ，年底集團作帳行情以
及美國11月期中選後不確定性下降有望支撐台股多頭情緒 。群益投顧樂觀看待台股第四季表
現，有望上看53,000點。此外，彭博綜合統計台灣加權指數12個月目標價達59,688點。

就業增幅明顯放緩，降低Fed短期再升息急迫性
• 非農明顯降溫，勞動市場尚未出現全面惡化：9月非農僅增2.9萬人，遠低於預期，且7、8月合計下修6萬人；失業率升
至4.2%，但勞參率同步升至 61.8%，家戶調查就業增加 40.6萬人，顯示失業率上升部分來自勞動供給增加 ，其中營建、
製造業仍分別增加1.1萬及0.9萬人，平均工時維持34.4小時，數據呈現招聘動能明顯放慢，但尚未出現全面性就業惡化。
• 薪資增速放緩，Fed短期政策壓力下降：9月平均時薪月增僅0.1%、年增3.0%，均低於前值；同時8月PCE年增3.4%、
核心PCE年增3.0%，顯示薪資通膨壓力有所緩和 ，但整體物價仍高於Fed 2%目標，在9月Fed升息1碼後，非農報告降
低Fed在10月連續升息的必要性，市場焦點轉向年底通膨與AI投資驅動的產業動能。

• 投資建議：短期關注美國中短天期投資級債券 ，就業降溫與薪資放緩有助降低短端利率上行壓力 ，但通膨仍高於目標 ，
因此不宜過度 預期長天期利率快速回落；股市則可續關注 AI基礎建設與資本支出相關產業 ，因營建及製造業就業仍具
韌性，顯示企業投資動能尚未明顯轉弱。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics， 2026.10.3

消費動能回升、通膨低於預期，內需韌性與物價壓力並存
• PCE低於預期、通膨降溫仍待確認：美國8月PCE物價指數月增0.3%、年增3.4%，核心PCE月增0.2%、年增3.0%，均低於
市場預期；其中核心PCE較修訂後7月的3.3%下降，顯示物價壓力有所緩解，但仍高於Fed 2%目標，此次年度更新回溯修
訂2021年以來資料，亦使歷史基期重新調整，後續仍須觀察通膨下降是否能延續。
• 消費回升、所得增幅偏緩：8月個人所得月增0.2%，個人消費支出（PCE）月增0.9%、實質消費成長0.6%，顯示內需仍具
韌性；惟消費增幅明顯高於所得 ，個人儲蓄率由7月4.6%降至4.1%，為2022年11月以來低點，反映家庭可支應消費的財
務緩衝持續縮小，後續消費動能仍須觀察。
• 後市觀察：PCE低於預期後，市場對10月再升息的預期明顯降溫，但長端美債殖利率仍受通膨、財政供給與期限溢價牽動

而維持高檔；建議可聚焦獲利成長與現金流穩健的優質大型成長股與短天期美債以掌握收益 ，同時留意儲蓄率下滑與 9月
消費者信心低迷可能抑制後續消費動能，動態調整部位並嚴控風險。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Economic Analysis， 2026.10.1

能源與AI推升長端利率，融資成本壓力延續
• 長端殖利率上行 、期限溢價成重要推力 ：近期
美債呈現短端與長端走勢分化 ，雖市場對 10月
Fed再升息預期因 8月PCE低於預期而明顯降溫 ，
10年期及30年期的美債殖利率仍偏高位 ，反映
通膨風險 、財政融資需求與期限溢價上升 ，長
天期無風險利率維持高檔 ，並推升房貸及企業
融資成本。
• 能源與AI投資推升通膨及資金需求 ：油價維持
高檔增加通膨黏著風險 ，AI基礎建設投資則帶
動企業資本支出與融資需求 ，兩者共同提高長
期資金需求；在財政赤字與美債供給仍大的環
境下，長端殖利率可能維持較高水準 ，進而提
高股票估值折現率及高槓桿企業的融資壓力。
• 後市觀察： 短期持續觀察油價 、10年及30年期
美債殖利率與期限溢價變化；債券配置仍建議

降低長天期債券部位以降低利率風險 ，優先布
局短天期高品質債券獲取穩健息收 ，股票則聚
焦具穩健現金流 、低財務槓桿與定價能力的企
業，以降低高利率環境下的估值與融資風險。

美光最新一季財報與財測 2026會計年度Q4實際 市場預期 2027會計年度Q1財測

AI需求大增，美光財測超過市場預期
• AI記憶體需求強勁，美光財測顯示景氣延續：美光科技9/30盤後公布財報，2026財年的第四季營收達542.3億美元，年增

379%，調整後EPS 33.42美元，均優於市場預期；公司並預估2027會計年度第一季營收615億美元、調整後EPS 38.15美元，
分別高於市場預期的567.7億美元及36.02美元。強勁財測反映AI資料中心持續推升HBM、伺服器DRAM及高速SSD需求。
隨Amazon、Alphabet等大型科技企業持續擴大AI基礎建設投資，記憶體需求仍具成長動能。
• 長約與產品升級，支撐中長期業績：美光目前已簽署36份多年期供應協議，涵蓋約三分之一營收並延伸至2030年，有助降
低記憶體產業週期波動；同時持續布局HBM4、HBM4E及高容量DDR5等產品。公司2026會計年度自由現金流達590億美元，
並預期2027、2028會計年度供需將進一步緊俏。整體而言，AI需求已由單一HBM擴展至伺服器記憶體與儲存產品，美光財
測亦顯示景氣延續性仍強，後續市場焦點將轉向擴產速度、資本支出及AI需求成長能否維持。
資料來源：美光，彭博，鉅亨網，2026.10.1

• AI驅動獲利成長，企業基本面仍具支撐：美國企業獲利維持強勁，背後主要受經濟展望穩健及AI資本支出熱潮支撐。從獲利
來看，市場共識預期標普500企業EPS成長率2026年達28%，2027年及2028年則分別為20%及16%，雖呈現逐步降溫，但
仍維持雙位數增長。其中，AI基礎建設與大型雲端業者仍是重要驅動來源，2026年對EPS成長的貢獻合計約19%，2027、
2028年仍分別貢獻14%及11%。
• 獲利上修降低估值壓力：標普500預期本益比已由約23倍降至19倍，且並非主要來自股價修正，而是企業獲利預期上調所致。
雖然未來EPS成長率若由高檔回落，市場仍可能面臨評價修正壓力，但在企業獲利維持成長、AI投資持續挹注及估值已較高
點收斂的情況下，標普500基本面仍具支撐。因此，短期應留意獲利增速放緩與市場持倉偏高所帶來的波動，但從獲利成長

與估值兩大面向觀察，標普500仍具中長期配置價值。

BCI:企業利潤前瞻指數 BCI:企業銷售前瞻指數
2026年製造業PMI 2024年 2025年 2023年
生產回升與價格上漲同步推動中國PMI改善
• 生產回升與價格上漲同步推動 中國PMI改善： 9月製造業PMI升至50.1%，較前月回升0.3個百分點，重返榮枯線以上，顯示
製造業景氣有所改善 ，主要受生產活動回暖及價格上漲帶動 ，中小企業景氣同步改善 ，但仍低於榮枯線，大型企業表現相對
較佳，高技術製造、裝備製造維持擴張 ，消費品PMI升至50.7%，重回景氣區間 ，另一方面，原材料及能源價格上漲推升企
業成本，上下游獲利差距持續擴大 。 非製造業PMI則由49.0%升至50.2%，服務業受節慶效應帶動回溫 ，建築業則受財政資

金投入，及基建建設推進而改善，不過需求復甦仍偏溫和，企業成本壓力尚未明顯減輕。
• 結論：第四季隨著基建投資、財政支出及房市支持政策逐步落地，經濟可望獲得支撐，但10月受長假因素影響，PMI可能短
期承壓。

台灣景氣燈號連9紅 台股Q4有望突破5萬點大關
• 8月景氣對策燈號續亮紅燈： 綜合判斷分數41分，較前月持平，對應的燈號仍是代表景氣熱絡的紅燈 ，連續第9個月亮出紅
燈，追平史上最長「連紅」紀錄，顯示國內經濟維持成長。國發會表示，AI需求令台廠積極擴廠，加上政府推動AI新十大建
設，民間投資有望維持動能。此外，受惠財富效應、企業調薪及就業市場穩定，民間消費有望持續成長。
• 法人看好台股年底前站上5萬點：根據歷史統計第四季台股上漲的機率接近八成 ，除了AI投資趨勢未減，年底集團作帳行情
以及美國11月期中選後不確定性下降有望支撐台股多頭情緒 。群益投顧樂觀看待台股第四季表現 ，有望上看53,000點。此
外，彭博綜合統計台灣加權指數12個月目標價達59,688點。
• 觀點：近期台股站上48,000點整數關卡，總市值達5.3兆美元，讓台股耀升成為全球第四大市值股市 ，顯示台股在AI浪潮下

的得天獨厚。年底前仍須留意中東衝突進展，若能源通膨壓力持續，聯準會升息預期恐為股市帶來波動，但考慮AI科技持續
發酵，建議投資人續抱台股，並以定期不定額的策略紀律加碼，目標追求中長期完整的AI趨勢回報。
資料來源：國發會，2026.09.29。

外匯對沖視角—美債對沖後殖利率屬全球最低之一
• 美國國債殖利率真的高嗎？在當前全球總體經濟環境下，美國國債殖利率雖維持在數十年歷史高點附近，但若單憑名目殖利
率來判斷美債的吸引力，容易忽視匯率對沖與各國財政風險帶來的溢價差異。
• 美元視角下的殖利率比較： 若將其他已開發市場的主權債務 (如德債、日債、英債)完全對沖回美元，美債在殖利率曲線上多
數期限的實際殖利率，其實與德債並列全球最低水準。相反地，日本國債與英國國債經匯率對沖回美元後的收益率反而更高。
• 跨貨幣資產交換 (ASW)的真實回報： 以10年期主權債的對沖跨貨幣資產交換計算：美國 10年期國債有效殖利率約 4.85%，
日本10年期國債(對沖至美元)有效殖利率約5.06%，德國10年期國債(對沖至美元)有效殖利率約4.51%，這表明美國政府發

債的真實對沖融資成本其實已相對精實，且符合國際市場對各國財政基本面的合理定價。
對沖至美元後的10年期主權債殖利率

財政基本面與信用風險—殖利率溢價反映國家債務比重
• 從歐元計價或信用風險的角度來比較 ，美債的定價邏輯非常符合總體經濟基本面： 儘管債務以歐元計價時 ，10年期美債殖
利率比同期限德國國債高出約30個基點，這反映出德國的債務佔GDP比重僅約65%，而美國則高達127%，因此美債承擔了
適度的財政溢價。儘管義大利的債務佔GDP比重高達140%且信用前景較遜色，但在統一貨幣基準下，義大利國債殖利率仍
較美國國債高出60個基點，這顯示市場並未過度壓低美債信用，美債依然享有優質避險資產的溢價優勢。
• 海外私人投資者的資金流向：近一年海外私人機構在美債加碼速度放緩，乃通膨環境下全球其他資產選擇增加，及過去升息
波段導致帳面按市值計價虧損 。然而歐洲投資人(持有一半以上的海外美債)目前仍持有龐大部位，並在等待適當的加碼時機。

• 小結與投資建議：在經過外匯對沖後，美債收益率在發達國家主權債中處於相對低位，發行成本合理且具競爭力。美債殖利
率高於德債低於義債 ，精確地反映美國政府債務負擔率 (127%)與總體經濟穩健度 。在全球公債遭遇拋售潮與通膨陰影下 ，
美債因利率期間結構與匯率優勢 ，今年以美元計價總回報優於多數 G7主權債，儘管海外私人機構投資人正在等待長端利率
落底訊號以重返市場，對美元資金或以美元計價為主的投資人而言，當前美債名目殖利率仍處於數十年高位，具備相當良好
的長期鎖利價值，但價格波動風險考量下，建議債券佈局仍以中短天期為主。
歐元計價美債殖利率高於德但低於義

亞洲晶⽚債券跑輸指數，評級保持穩健
• 亞洲晶⽚債券跑輸指數，評級保持穩健：儘管AI熱潮推動收⼊創下紀錄 ，但SK海力⼠和台積電的美元債 ，今年以來的表現仍
略遜於彭博亞洲美元信用債總回報指數 ，主因為大規模資本支出擴張 ，以及潛在的投⼊資本回報率下降令 其利差承壓，美國
雲服務巨頭創紀錄的 AI支出計畫加劇了壓力 ，儘管如此 SK 海力⼠和台積電仍保持較強的高評級信用實力 ，台積電計畫投
⼊600-640億美元資本支出 ，SK海力⼠的資本支出則處於 40兆韓圜區間。兩家公司穩健的資產負債表 、合計共1,260億美元
的淨現金、強勁的經營性現金流，以及在先進製程，和HBM晶⽚領域的市場領先地位，為上述支出提供支撐。
• 投資建議：現階段台積電與SK海力⼠美元債最大的問題不是信用風險 ，而是估值與利差壓力 ，短期可能因AI資本支出過大，

及市場情緒波動而表現落後指數 ，但以兩家公司強大的現金流 、淨現金部位及產業領導地位來看 ，信用品質仍屬亞洲投資級
債中的優質標的，建議利差擴大時分批布局 。

顧 • 企業獲利成長將支撐股價上行：標普500指數的長期走勢與企業每股盈餘（EPS）高度連動。高盛預估，標普500企業EPS將
於2026年達375美元，並在2027年進一步升至415美元，對應年增率約36%與11%。在利率維持高檔、金融條件偏緊的環境
下，企業獲利仍持續處於成長軌道，將為股市提供穩健的中期上行動能。
• 本益比回落提供潛在布局契機：根據FactSet數據，標普500企業第三季整體獲利預計成長30.6%。其中，資訊科技類股獲利
成長預估高達66.5%，領先能源以外的其他產業；同時，該類股預估本益比已由今年高點回落，在獲利上修與估值修正同步
發生的背景下，浮現具吸引力的中期布局機會。
• 小結：短期市場仍可能受地緣政治與利率預期擾動，但中期趨勢仍由企業獲利擴張所主導。建議投資人採取分批布局科技股

策略，聚焦具備AI受益結構、資本支出能見度高及現金流穩健的龍頭企業。

市场有风险，投资需谨慎。投资者不应将本报告作为作出投资决策的唯一参考因素，亦不应认为本报告可以取代自己的判断。在决定投资前，如有需要，投资者务必向专业人士咨询并谨慎决策。
（一）本轮PPI修复成色
2026年10月，PPI同比上涨5.7%，总量回升背后行业价格修复仍明显分化。以2025年7月为观察起点，
40个工业大类行业中，22个行业价格上涨，占比55%，上涨覆盖略超过半数；其余18个行业价格继续下降，
主要包括汽车、通用设备、专用设备，以及食品、服装、医药等中下游行业。
价格回升的幅度与历史跌幅的收复程度也存在差异，目前仍有30个行业的价格指数低于2022年10月水
平，占比75%。部分行业虽然已经反弹，但尚不足以弥补此前累积的跌幅，多数行业价格仍处于相对低位。

风险提示：国际大宗商品价格大幅波动；终端需求恢复不及预期；上游涨价向中下游传导不畅；行业权重及黄金贡献估算
存在偏差，实际价格走势偏离翘尾测算假设。

（三）CPI修复进入平台期
10月CPI和核心CPI同比分别上涨0.7%和0.8%，均较9月回落0.2个百分点。核心CPI涨幅回落约六成来
自黄金支撑减弱；剔除黄金后的超核心价格指标同比约为0.84%，同样处于低增速平台。

回顾本轮修复，2025年下半年至2026年初，剔除食品、能源和黄金后的价格指标明显回升，表明价
格改善并非完全依赖特殊项目；但2026年春季以来，该指标大致趋稳，终端价格修复进入平台期，进一
步上行动力仍显不足。同期，PPI同比滚动均值持续上升，工业品价格走强尚未伴随终端基础消费价格的
同步加速，二者的分化尚无收敛迹象。但基础消费价格保持相对稳定，也暂未显示终端需求压力持续加
深。

（二）涨价动能与正翘尾支撑短期PPI
短期内，PPI同比仍有两方面支撑：一是当期涨价动能尚未消退，10月PPI环比上涨0.7%，显示工业品
价格仍在上行；二是年内已经发生的价格上涨，将通过正翘尾对2027年同比读数形成支撑。
假设2026年11-12月价格维持10月水平，按现有口径测算，2027年1月PPI翘尾因素可拉动同比上涨近5

个百分点。正翘尾对2027年PPI同比的较强支撑可延续至一季度，4月明显减弱，7-8月阶段性回升，9月大
幅消退。这一假设用于衡量截至10月的既有涨价的滞后影响。考虑到当前价格上涨动能仍在，将年末价格
维持在10月水平作为现阶段的基准情景，具有一定合理性。
从结构看，按底稿行业权重近似测算，在2026年末价格维持当前水平的假设下，2027年1月，石油、
煤炭及其他燃料加工业,化学原料及化学制品制造业,计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业,煤炭开采和洗
选业的翘尾贡献分别约为1.37、1.08、0.63和0.62个百分点。四个行业权重合计约24.7%，贡献合计约
3.70个百分点，贡献当期PPI同比翘尾的74.2%。因此，短期PPI同比读数有较强支撑，但价格修复基础尚
待巩固。悲观情形，若能源化工价格回落的同时，中下游价格未能接续修复，并伴随降价范围扩大和订单
走弱，则即使PPI同比处于高位，也需关注价格下行压力重新累积。

2026 年 10 月 30 日

調査レポート

2026 年 7～9 月期の GDP（1 次速報）予測

～ほぼゼロ成長も、緩やかな景気回復は継続～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田 隼平

2026 年 11 月 16 日に内閣府が公表する 7～9 月期の実質 GDP（1 次速報値）は、前期比＋0.0%（前期比年
率換算＋0.1%）と予測する。設備投資や政府消費の増加に支えられ、かろうじて 4 四半期連続のプラス成長を
維持したものの、個人消費や輸出が前期の一時的な押し上げ要因の剥落もあって減少したことで、ほぼゼロ成長
にとどまったと見込まれる。なお、輸入が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直したことで外需寄与度は大きめのマイナスと
なるものの、公的在庫のプラス寄与により、おおむね相殺されたと考えられる。 
また、名目 GDP は、前期比＋0.9%（年率換算＋3.8%）となったと見込まれる。コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進
む中で、GDP デフレーターは前年比＋2.7%（季節調整済前期比では＋0.9%）と高めの伸びが続いた。 
今回、7～9 月期の実質 GDP はほぼゼロ成長にとどまる見込みだが、イラン情勢の悪化という逆風を踏まえれ
ば、国内景気は底堅さを維持している 。先行きについては、輸入コストの高止まりに伴う価格転嫁の進展が景気
の重しとなる可能性はあるものの、良好な雇用・所得環境や企業業績の改善を背景に内需の回復基調が維持さ
れることなどから、国内景気は緩やかな回復を続けると期待される。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度 
（出所）内閣府「四半期別 GDP 速報」により作成

### Page 2

2 / 2

需要項目別の動向は以下の通りである。

① 実質個人消費（前期比 －0.1%） 
 良好な所得環境が維持され る中、消費者マインドも持ち直しへ向かったものの、前期に 耐久財
が自動車やエアコンを中心に 一時的な要因によって増加した反動 が生じた とみられる 。価格転
嫁の進展による 物価上昇 、地震や台風といった自然災害の発生 も重しとなった 可能性がある 。 
② 実質住宅投資（前期比 －0.3%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数 が横ばいとなる中 、建築コストが上昇していることもあり、 住
宅投資は小幅ながら減少が続いたとみられる 。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比 ＋0.9%） 
 企業の投資意欲は 省力化やデジタル需要 などに支えられて 堅調であり 、設備投資は 3 四半期
ぶりに増加 したと見込まれる 。機械設備投資の供給側統計である資本財総供給（除く輸送機械）
も 7～9 月期は前期比 ＋5.3%と増加した。 
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度 －0.0%ポイント ） 
 前期に 供給制約懸念の高まりを受けて、メーカーが製品在庫などの積み増しを進めた 反動もあ
り、7～9 月期はマイナス 寄与になったと考えられる 。 
 なお、公的在庫 について は、政府による代替調達の進展により 石油備蓄の 減少ペースが大きく鈍
化したことから、前期比寄与度 ＋0.5% ポイントと プラス寄与になったと みられる 。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費支出（前期比＋ 0.3%） 
 高齢化が進む中、医療費 ・介護費を中心に 底堅く推移 したと予測する。  
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比 －2.7%） 
 建築・土木コストの上昇が続いている中、 7～8 月の建設総合統計（出来高ベース：公共） は弱
めの動きとなった ことから 、実質公共投資は 2 四半期 連続で 減少したと見込まれる。  
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度 －0.5%ポイント 、実質輸出：前期比 －0.5%、実質輸入：同 ＋1.7%） 
 輸出は 前期比 －0.5%と減少したと予測する。財は 堅調な 米国向け や中東向けの持ち直しを受
けて増加 した一方、サービスは 前期に 研究開発サービスが一時 的な 要因により上振れた 反動
で減少 したとみられる。  
 輸入 は前期比 ＋1.7%と増加したと 予測する。 特に財が鉱物性燃料 を中心に 持ち直した。ただ
し、サービス については 減少した 可能性がある 。 
 結果 、輸出から輸入を差し引いた 外需 の寄与度は －0.5%ポイント とマイナス になった と見込ま
れる。

Document 145: 261109_daiwa_ Taiwan Consumer Discretionary Global PMX

### Page 1

Taiwan Consumer Discretionary

What’s new: We attended PMX’s investor conference after the company 
released its 3Q26 results on 9 November 2026. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call 
as we see better-than-expected demand for its semiconductor, AI server 
and medical segments.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results missed mainly on FX loss. PMX’s 
3Q26 revenue of TWD2,417m (+5.9% QoQ, +24.6% YoY) beat our 
estimate by 8.5% and was in line with the Bloomberg consensus. Its gross 
margin came in at 23.9% vs. 24.6% in 2Q26 and 21.7% in 3Q25, missing 
our estimate (24.2%) and the consensus (25.1%), mainly due to some one-
off costs ramping up at the new Vietnam plant. With higher-than-expected 
opex, operating profit of TWD340m (+10.0% QoQ, +54.4% YoY) missed 
our and the consensus estimates by 1.3% and 6.0%, respectively. With an 
FX loss of TWD23m, its net profit of TWD271m (+13.3% QoQ, +0.1% YoY) 
missed our and consensus estimates by 11.1% and 10.2%, respectively. 
EPS was TWD2.36 in 3Q26. PMX’s 3Q26 revenue breakdown is as 
follows: auto sales at 49% (+13% YoY), semiconductor, AI server & other 
sales at 20% (+66% YoY), storage device sales at 13% (+25% YoY), 
medical sales at 10% (+13% YoY), and sports sales at 8% (+42% YoY). 
PMX also posted October 2026 monthly sales of TWD816m (+3.3% MoM 
and +33.8% YoY; +22.0% YoY for 10M26), which accounted for 35.6% and 
31.3%, respectively, of our previous and the consensus forecasts of 4Q26 
revenue.

Segment transformation continues with upbeat guidance for 4Q26 
and 2027. For 2026, PMX maintained its overall 20-25% YoY revenue 
growth guidance with semiconductor, AI server & others segment growing 
by at least 100% YoY. For 2027, management expects revenue to grow by 
20%+ YoY with the semiconductor, AI server & others segment to grow by 
100%+ YoY; medical, by 50%+ YoY; storage device, by 10% YoY; auto, flat; 
and sports to decline by 5%. We revised down our 2026-28E EPS by 0.3-
8.5% after factoring in its 3Q26 results.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with an unchanged 
12M TP of TWD205, now based on a target PER of 17x (from 18x; above 
the 15x average of its past-3-year range of 11-25x) on our 1-year forward 
EPS forecast. We lower the PER target slightly on its decelerating earnings 
momentum. Key downside risks: lower than-expected global auto, medical, 
and AI server component demand; worse-than-expected gross margin.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 18-22% below the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely as we are more conservative about PMX’s revenue and 
operating margins.

9 November 2026

3Q26 results review: weaker-than-expected margins

➢ 3Q26 net profit missed our and consensus estimates by 10-11% 
➢ Semiconductor, AI server and Others segments to support growth  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call with unchanged 12M TP of TWD205

Company profile 
Founded in 1987, Global PMX provides mechanical parts for automotive, medical equipment, and 
storage device components. Its major automotive clients include Continental, Bosch, BorgWarner, ZF, 
Hilite and Tesla. Other major clients are Seagate, Medtronic, and Zimmer.

### Page 5

5

Global PMX (4551 TT): 9 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson of PMX are not the same individual. The board includes 3
independent directors, including 1 female independent director. The Chairman has held this
position at PMX for more than 20 years, indicating the company's strong management capability
and leadership, in our view.
Capital management 1
PMX's payout ratio was 80-90% before 2018, but dropped to 29-69% in 2019-25, which we
attribute to its acquisitions over the past few of years (eg, merger with Sixxon in 2020). We view
these acquisitions as reasonable capital management as PMX’s product line expansion has led
to lower risk exposure due to diversification, helping PMX's 2020-24 revenue to stay relatively
intact.
Related party &
transaction 2 No major revenue from related-party transactions in 2020-25, and we observed no severe issues.
S Product quality & safety 2
PMX holds numerous international product quality certifications, including ISO9002, QS9000 and
ISO/TS16949, which help to regulate the production phases, from development to quality control
and sales. Besides, products made by PMX are 100% compliant with laws/regulations and
market expectations on health and safety, so we believe that product quality and safety concerns
are not a high risk for PMX.
S Materials sourcing & efficiency 2 Global PMX's supply conditions of raw materials (mainly copper and steel) are stable, and its suppliers are required to sign the company’s Supplier Commitment Statement. Therefore, we believe materials sourcing and efficiency will be under control going forward.
S Product design & lifecycle management 2 PMX has adopted strategies that avoid price competition by targeting production of higher value- added products (including auto, medical, semiconductor, and green energy products). Thus, we believe that PMX is continually improving its product lifecycle management.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

M
Update
Lite-On Technology  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +6% MoM/+32%
YoY
In this report, we focus on Lite-On's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales came in at NT$20,071mn (+6% MoM/+32% YoY), driven by the
server PSU/BBU and opto semiconductors (couplers and indicators for
industrial automation, new energy and AI applications).
• QTD revenue reached 33% of both MSe at NT$60,395mn (+5% QoQ/+36%

YoY), and consensus at NT$60,353mn (+5% QoQ/+36% YoY).
• By segment, Cloud & AIoT/Optoelectronics/IT&CE accounted for
66%/13%/21% of the monthly sales, respectively, implying growth of +19%/-
1%/-17% MoM and  +71%/+7%/-16% YoY.
Our view:
• We think its 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We believe Lite-On will grow along with the AI capex cycle driven by its
power-related offerings, though execution and progress with new customers

remain key to watch.
• Stay EW.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

잠시 쉬어갑니다

3Q26 Review 
기아는 3Q26P 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.4% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회했다. 영업이익 증
감 요인은 환율 효과(+5,290억원), 물량 효과(+4,420억원), 믹스 효과(-4,650억원), 
인센티브/가격 효과(-7,270억원), 재료비 영향(-3,680억원), 기타(+4,130억원)이다. 
판매 성장에도 높아진 수익성 부담 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.9% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.1만대
(+8.8%)를 기록했다. 미국 HEV와 서유럽 EV 판매가 각각 118%, 163% 증가하며 
글로벌 점유율은 4.0%까지 확대됐다. 다만, 판매 성장에도 원화 강세와 원자재 가격 
상승, EV 비중 확대에 따른 믹스 부담으로 수익성은 전분기 대비 하락했다. 유럽 내 
EV 판매가 늘어나면서 믹스가 악화되었으며, 전쟁으로 인한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 
재료비 부담은 전년 대비 약 3,700억 증가했다.  
연간 판매목표 335만대는 유지했으나, 4분기 수익성의 급격한 반등은 어려울 전망이
다. 향후 미국 텔루라이드·스포티지 HEV 공급 확대와 유럽 EV 판매 증가에 따른 고정
비 흡수가 수익성 회복의 핵심이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 155,000원으로 하향 
기아에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하고, 실적 추정치 하향을 반영해 목표주가를 
155,000원으로 하향한다. 재료비 부담, 환율 효과 축소, 예상보다 큰 믹스 악화로 단
기 이익 눈높이 조정은 불가피하다. 다만 산업수요 둔화에도 주요 시장에서 점유율을 
확대되고 있다는 점은 긍정적이다. 현재 배당 수익률은 7% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높
은 배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82만원에서 64만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

작성자(한승한)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

Document 264: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

(329180) 
HD 현대중공업 
2026.10.29

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 610,000원(하향) 
 현재주가 285,500원(10/28)

 3Q26 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2%yoy), 영업이익은 8,992억원
(+61.3%yoy, OPM 15.6%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센서스 9,792억원 하회.  
 평균 환율이 약 79원 하락하며 영업이익 약 450억원이 줄었고, 조업
일수는 47일로 통상 계절성 대비 4일 추가 감소해 약 500억원의 이
익 감소 효과 발생. 임단협 추가 비용의 약 75%도 3분기에 선반영
(잔여분은 4분기 반영). 일회성 요인을 제외하면  이익 체력은 견조. 
 동사의 조선 부문 매출액은 4조 3,988억원(+44.0%yoy), 영업이익은 
6,463억원(+62.8%yoy, OPM 14.7%)을 기록. 상선 OPM은 14.7%로 
2Q26(17.8%) 대비 하락했으나 대부분 외생변수 영향. 3Q26 대형선 매출 
중 2023년 수주분 비중은 45%, 2024~2025년 수주분은 51%. 고선가 
물량의 매출 비중이 높아지는 2027년까지 수익성 개선 여력은 충분.  
 9월 말 누적 상선 수주는 138.0억달러로 연간 목표의 120%, 엔진기계는 
41.4억달러로 155%를 달성했으나 함정(13%)과 해양(5%)은 부진. LNG선
은 중국 조선소의 저가 수주와 발주 둔화로 선가가 일부 하락. 선주들이 현 
선가를 저점으로 인식하며 발주 협의를 재개하는 분위기. 북미 LNG 프로젝
트 FID가 이어지고 있어 2027년 LNG선 발주 회복 기대. 
 함정 부문은 매출 2,570억원(-14.6%qoq)으로 줄었으나, 수출 비중 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선으로 영업이익 386억원(+114%qoq, OPM 15.0%)을 기록. 
해양플랜트는 매출액 3,799억원(+35.3%yoy), 영업이익 1,265억원(OPM 
33.3%). C/O 180억원을 제외해도 Ruya 공정 본격화와 Trion 고수익 물량
으로 높은 수익성 유지. 2027년 일부 프로젝트 인도로 매출 감소가 예상되
나, LNG선·블록 물량 투입으로 고정비 부담을 완화할 계획. 신규 해상변전
소(OSS) 계약을 앞두고 있으며, 2027년 초 추가 수주 발표도 기대. 
 주목할 부분은 엔진기계. 영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)으로 높은 D/F 
엔진 비중을 바탕으로 견조한 수익성 유지. 육상발전용 힘센엔진은 올해   
3GW를 확보하며 2029년까지 계획 캐파 수준의 물량을 이미 채움. 생산 
예상 물량은 2028년 약 1.5GW, 2029년 약 3GW이며, 신규 공장 구축 이
후 연간 생산능력은 약 4GW로 확대 예정. 북미 Hyperscaler와의 직접 협
상이 늘고 있으며, 힘센엔진을 활용한 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)로 FDC 시장
까지 대응 가능. AI 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2028년 이후 새로운 이익 축. 
 미국에서는 Kiewit과 합작법인 설립 계약 체결. 군수지원함 등 지원함 사업
부터 추진하며, 현지 조선소 건설 과정에서 HII에 선박 블록을 공급하는 방
안도 협의 중. 구체적인 투자계획은 2026년 말까지 확정 목표. 국내에서는 
핵추진잠수함 초기 예산이 2027년 반영되며 동사도 사업 참여를 준비 중. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 61만원을 제시. 고선가 물량 매출 확
대와 육상발전용 엔진 성장으로 피크아웃을 이겨낼 굴지의 조선사.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We've lifted our Q3 expectation for real PCE on incoming data and a slightly firmer consumer
backdrop in the wake of recent revisions.
Consumer spending looks firmer in the third quarter, and we now expect real PCE to rise at a 3.4%
annualized clip. This upward revision reflects recent data, with August consumption coming in firm
with real spending rising by the fastest monthly clip in nearly two years, but also reflects a slightly

firmer consumer backdrop in the wake of the BEA's annual revisions released late September. Although
revisions did not materially alter spending, they lifted the level of household income in recent years,
which also boosted the personal saving rate. The trajectory of real income growth remains weak, but
the consumer still looks to be in a slightly stronger financial position than previously believed, which
helps explain recent resilience in spending and suggests consumers may have a bit more runway ahead.

Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build out will lead to another surge in business fixed investment spending in Q3, though the
effect on headline growth will be partly offset by stronger imports.
Business fixed investment spending continues to be supported primarily by the high-tech buildout.
We forecast real equipment investment to rise at around a 16% annualized clip in Q3, but part of
this boost to headline GDP growth will be offset by stronger import growth that is facilitating the

build out. Net imports of computers are tracking to surge in the third quarter, which will provide a
sizable lift to information processing equipment investment, or around half of overall equipment.
And while non-defense capital goods shipments (including aircraft) slipped in August, the near-term
growth trajectory remains supportive of a pickup in equipment. The rebuild in inventories we have
been anticipating is also tracking to be a bit stronger as businesses replenish drawn down product.

Investment: Residential
• The abrupt rise in mortgage rates is likely to dampen residential investment over the next few
quarters. Residential spending should find support next year as longer-term interest rates
moderate somewhat.
We are unlikely to see another positive residential investment reading in Q3. Rising mortgage rates

have sent a chill over builder outlooks, pushing sales expectations to their weakest level in more than
three years. While builders have responded with deeper price cuts, affordability pressures continue
to weigh on single-family sales and construction outlays. Apartment construction is simultaneously
soft amid rising financing costs and modest rent growth, and home improvement spending remains
constrained by high materials costs and interest rates. While residential investment is likely to remain

4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
a drag this year, we expect a slight moderation in longer-term interest rates in 2027, which would
provide a more supportive backdrop.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• We have not made material changes to our nonresidential structures forecast. Despite elevated
interest rates, data center and related spending should continue to support structures investment
through our forecast horizon.
Nonresidential structures spending has accelerated sharply, overwhelmingly driven by data centers

and electric power facilities. As data center construction expands geographically and demand rises
for semiconductors, high-tech capex has also started to generate positive spillovers for warehousing
and manufacturing construction. This buildout, which appears relatively insensitive to interest rates,
should be enough to sustain solid growth in structures investment even as spending across most
other categories remains essentially flat. Domestic energy is another green shoot, with elevated prices

supporting ongoing growth in active rotary rigs.

Net Exports & Trade
• An expected drag from trade in Q3 got larger in light of a near-record trade deficit in August, net
exports is now expected to lop off three full percentage points from third quarter growth.

Exports increased in August for the first time in four months, but the increase was more than offset by
an even larger increase in imports. The upshot was a widening in the trade deficit to a near-record gap
of more than $105 billion. Going into the report, we had a 1.9 percentage point drag from net exports
penciled in for Q3 GDP; we revised that to a full 3.0 percentage points drag in light of the record gap.
Even a modest narrowing in the final months of the year would likely mean a lift to headline GDP in

Q4, but the real story here is that trade will remain a headwind throughout the forecast period owing
largely to surging imports of tech-related products amid the AI boom.
Labor Market
• The jobs market remains stable. We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end the year at
4.2% and hold near that level in 2027.
We modestly revised down our near-term payroll growth forecast following the September jobs
report, but otherwise we have left our labor market outlook unchanged. Measures of labor demand

growth continue to point to a stable labor market. Job openings have inched higher on a year-ago
basis but remain broadly steady while initial jobless claims, WARN notices and Challenger job cut
announcements continue to signal low layoffs. Labor force participation appears to be improving after
its sharp pullback in the first half of the year. Looking ahead, we expect payroll growth to average
around 60K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate of breakeven job growth. That should

Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
keep the unemployment rate near 4.2% in 2027 and leave labor market conditions broadly consistent
with "full employment." Notably, our expectation for labor cost growth of ~3.4% is the reason we
continue to reiterate that labor cost growth is not a contributor to the excess inflation seen in the
data.
Inflation
• We expect the core PCE deflator to rise 3.2% and 2.6% Q4/Q4 in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Headline inflation remains heavily influenced by developments in the Middle East, with higher oil

prices lifting gasoline and diesel prices. Food inflation has been tame this year but looks poised to
strengthen as higher transportation and commodity prices work their way through supply chains.
Beyond food and energy, price pressures remain concentrated in a handful of categories, most notably
airfares and AI-related tech goods. Core goods inflation is showing signs of stabilizing, as waning tariff-
related price pressures have been offset by resilient demand tied to the AI buildout. We expect some

improvement here in 2027. Still-low shelter inflation and cooler price growth for airfares and health
care should help bring about some moderation in core services inflation next year. We expect the core
PCE deflator to rise 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to 2.6% in 2027.

Fiscal Policy
• Fiscal year (FY) 2026 ended on September 30, and the federal government tallied a $2.0 trillion
budget deficit (6.5% of GDP). We project the deficit to be unchanged in dollar terms in FY 2027

and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
• Another continuing resolution to extend government funding past the December 11 deadline
seems like the most likely outcome at this time for the FY 2027 budget. It is possible Republicans
use the lame duck session of Congress to increase defense spending and raise the debt ceiling.
Looking ahead to the lame duck session of Congress that will follow the midterm elections, Congress
needs to pass a bill that extends government funding past December 11 to avert a government

shutdown. Another continuing resolution that punts the issue into Q1 seems like the most likely
outcome. Perhaps more interesting will be whether Republicans attempt to use budget reconciliation
to pass one more partisan piece of legislation. If they do, we suspect the two things at the top the
agenda will be more funding for national defense and immigration enforcement priorities as well as a
debt ceiling increase. For the latter, raising it in the lame duck would remove some leverage Democrats

could have in 2027 if they recapture one or both chambers. It is still very early, but we estimate the
debt ceiling "X date" by which Congress must act or face default is sometime in late Q3/early Q4 of
next year.
6 | Economics

### Page 7

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our base case forecast is for the FOMC to hike the fed funds rate by 25 bps in December and
then keep the policy rate unchanged in 2027 at 4.00%-4.25%, with risks skewed toward additional
tightening.
As we go to print, the FOMC seems more likely than not to keep the fed funds rate unchanged in
October. But given the rationale under which the Fed started this tightening cycle and the forcefulness

with which Warsh has driven home his displeasure with elevated inflation, it seems entirely plausible
to us that October will be a live meeting. At a minimum, the recent inflation data seem to make a hike
in December a lock. Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our
expectation is that core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see signs of demand-
side inflation in the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation

data reset the clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates upside
risk to our base case for the fed funds rate.

International Developments
• Global growth remains resilient, leaving our 2026 and 2027 forecasts unchanged. Our inflation
forecasts are also unchanged, although risks of delayed price pass-through could prolong inflation
pressures into 2027.

• Monetary policy continues to lean toward tightening across advanced economies, while emerging
market central banks are increasingly cautious, either holding rates or easing more gradually as
inflation pressures rebuild.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside, complicating the monetary policy
outlook even as growth has proven more resilient than expected. Elevated energy prices, particularly
for refined petroleum products, persistent geopolitical uncertainty and El Niño-related food price

risks could prolong inflationary pressures into 2027. While second-round effects still remain relatively
contained, repeated supply shocks are testing central banks’ willingness to look through the temporary
price increases, particularly where inflation expectations remain elevated, even as higher government
bond yields are already contributing to tighter financial conditions.
Across advanced economies, central banks face increasingly difficult trade-offs as persistent inflation

pressures limit the scope for policy accommodation. The Eurozone remains particularly vulnerable
to energy supply disruptions, with elevated natural gas prices and low storage levels adding to
inflation risks. We continue to expect a 25 bps European Central Bank hike in December, followed by a
prolonged hold through 2027. Fiscal concerns are also adding to pressure, particularly in France, where
the selloff in government debt has pushed yields higher. In the U.K., resilient activity and renewed

Economics | 7

### Page 8

Monthly Economics
inflation pressures similarly underpin our expectation for a Bank of England hike in November,
followed by a hold through 2027.
Elsewhere, policy paths are becoming more differentiated. After four hikes this year brought the Cash
Rate to 4.60%, we expect the Reserve Bank of Australia to remain on hold, although renewed inflation
pressures or stronger-than-expected activity could prompt further tightening. We also still expect
the Bank of Canada to hold at 2.25%, with subdued growth weighing against energy-driven inflation

risks, although incoming inflation data over the coming weeks will be critical in determining whether
persistent price pressures warrant tightening monetary policy perhaps as early as the December
meeting. Japan remains an exception to the broader hold outlook: while the yen's recovery provides
some relief from imported inflation, elevated energy prices and import dependence continue to pose
upside risks. We expect the Bank of Japan to hike again by year-end and now expect an additional 25

bps hike in Q3-2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.
The emerging market story has increasingly shifted from easing toward patience or selective
tightening. In Asia, China's weak domestic demand and persistent property-sector strains continue to
warrant policy support, with AI-related exports providing some offset. We expect a more gradual pace
of Reserve Requirement Ratio cuts alongside targeted monetary and fiscal measures. India faces the

opposite challenge, with elevated food and energy prices and rupee depreciation strengthening the
case for further Reserve Bank of India tightening. In Latin America, peso depreciation and elevated
energy costs pose upside inflation risks for Mexico, although limited exchange-rate pass-through
should allow Banxico to remain on hold while policymakers monitor underlying inflation and inflation
expectations. In Brazil, election-related uncertainty and renewed inflation pressures are likely to delay

further easing, with cuts expected to resume in Q1-2027 once policymakers see more convincing
evidence of disinflation.

Document 411: STABLECOINS_ What next for crypto assets_ (2026-10-20)

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR
Timeline of select events

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 192026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.
Stablecoins payment use case is steadily growing

### Page 5

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer valueproposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

### Page 6

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FXand Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.
Should we explain how settlement work as it is very
interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?
Probably more relevant than having the stablecoin
market cap here I would say.
A more integrated post-trade process

### Page 7

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Corporates, card networks, and banks are integrating
stablecoins
Institutions are racing to capture stablecoin market share
– Card networks: Mastercard expanded its settlement capabilities to include
stablecoins, using USDC and Paxos-issued stablecoins.
– Institutions: Exploring stablecoins via developing in-house permissioned

blockchain experiments and/or exploring issuance of proprietary stablecoins.
– >140 companies and financial institutions helped launch Open USD
(OUSD) in Sep 2026. OUSD reached $799mn of supply in 2 weeks.
Signals shift toward consortium-backed/network-led digital dollar
models.
Are stablecoins posing a threat to traditional payment systems?
– Bullish: Stablecoins to surpass traditional rails by the 2030s (Chainalysis).
– ~17% of stablecoin-linked card volume is occurring across Visa’s

business and commercial card programs. Shows growing adoption of
stablecoins for settlement, treasury management and cross-border
commerce.
– Key concerns for banks: Fee pressure from stablecoin adoption →
bank outflows → higher reliance on wholesale funding → pressure on
net interest margins.
– Base case: However, Deutsche Bank equity analysts see more limited near-
term risk. Visa and Mastercard to remain dominant especially in advanced
markets.

– KYC, AML, sanctions screening, wallet monitoring, fraud controls and
jurisdiction-specific limits remain essential for banks.
Stablecoin transactions could match Visa and Mastercard transactions
sometime between 2031 and 2039 (Chainalysis).
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Chainalysis.
Shows projected years until crypto merchant transactions reach Visa scale.

### Page 9

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
GEOPOLITICS: Why stablecoins are a "Genius" idea for the US
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, US Treasury, Tether July 2026 report. Shows Major foreign holders of Treasury Securities.
Treasury data from June 2026.
Can sustain USD dominance
– 99% of stablecoins (by market cap) are USD-pegged.
– Stablecoins function primarily as a digital version of dollars

moving over blockchain rails, extending the dollar’s reach into
new digital environments.
– Reinforces dollar savings: The more firms invoice and transact in USD,
the more they save in USD assets. Stablecoins help preserve this
feedback loop by keeping businesses and individuals in dollar
payment rails.
Can support US debt, given share held by foreigners has fallen from
>50% in early 2000s to ~30% in early 2026.
– Stablecoin issuers total holdings: ~$170bn according to Allium x

Citizen.
– Tether: ~$141bn in direct and indirect exposure to US T-bills.
– Impact: BIS shows how $3.5bn inflow into dollar-backed stablecoins
lowers 3m T-bill yields by 2.5-3.5bp under normal conditions. Effect
more than doubles in periods of T-bill shortages.
– Forecasts: T-bills holding by stablecoin issuers could reach ~$1trn by
2028 (Treasury forecasts).
– Recent Fed SF research places it closer to $400bn by the end
of 2030, lower than the US government’s financing needs.

Stablecoin issuers’  demand for US debt could counterweight declining demand from China
Tether competes with Saudi Arabia and South Korea as major holders of US Treasuries
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, US Treasury Data. Shows Treasury
holdings, stablecoin issuers vs China.

### Page 10

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
US GENIUS ACT: Debate on rules around payment stablecoins one year on
GENIUS provisions
– Strict issuer requirements: Issuers can be private, non-bank entity, but must
meet strict federal or state requirements.
– Reserves: Requires 1:1 backing with US cash, 93 days T-bills, repos/reverse
repos, or money market funds holding those assets.
– AML/KYC/BSA compliance obligations to stablecoin issuers, aligning them

exams, and strong internal controls.
Next steps
– GENIUS takes effect no later than 18 Jan 2027, or 120 days after final
implementing regulations are issued.
– Ongoing pushback from US banks as to whether GENIUS ban on yield-
bearing stablecoins should extend to affiliate or third-party arrangements,
given many users do not hold stablecoins directly with the issuer.
– CLARITY ACT failed in Sep because of disagreements on how
exchanges/broader crypto market structure should handle stablecoins.

– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
There are two ways stablecoin yield can be paid; ongoing debate over the three-
party model
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Congressional Research Service.

RISKS: Still hotly discussed amongst policymakers and financial institutions
Bank liquidity risk
Loss of stable funding source for banks from deposit outflow (not offset by
partial flowback from stablecoin issuers’ reserve deposit)
System stability risk
Stablecoins can fail to maintain their $1 value during times of stress. Evidence shows
that USDC and Tether actually trade within 1% of their dollar value but prices can

diverge sharply during stress episodes.
Run on stablecoin issuer(s) in market stress / trust scenario triggering fire sales of
reserve assets and/or deposit withdrawals.

stablecoins, e.g. gold with only
small market share

REGULATORY COMPARISON
Overview of stablecoin regulation in the US, UK and EU UKEU US
Regime Maturity
Key Timelines
Regulatory
Framework
US
The U.S. Genius Act
EU
Markets in Crypto-Asset Regulation
(MiCAR)
Backing Assets*
• 100% backing assets in USD cash or short-term
Treasuries (≤93-day T-bills).
• Non-significant EMTs –At least 30% reserves in
same-currency bank deposits, remainder in HQLA

• Significant EMTs - At least 60% of the reserves in
bank deposits; remainder in HQLA
• Systemic stablecoins – 30% unremunerated BoE
deposits vs 70% in UK sovereign debt (HQLA)
• Non-systemic – Full 1:1 backing in HQLR assets,
minimum 5% in on demand deposits.
UK
Multi-issuance
Stance
• Rules are still being fine-tuned; no specific focus
yet on multi-issuance.
• Multi-issuance under active review; EC considers multi-
issuance permissible, an equivalence-based framework for

EU/third-country arrangements is under legislative
consideration.
• BoE considers multi-issuance unsuitable for systemic
UK use. Preference for single-issuance models backed
by single reserve (incl. Non-GPB denom. stablecoins) .
UK Cryptoasset Regime (FCA)
UK Systemic Stablecoins Regime (BoE)
Eligible Issuers
• Bank subsidiaries, federal nonbank licensees, or
state issuers with federal registration, with
enhanced oversight for >$10bn circulation.

• EMTs: EU-authorised credit institutions or authorised
electronic money institutions. Restrictions apply to
non-EU currency EMTs (exceeding MiCAR
thresholds) as a means of exchange
• FCA-authorised firms for non-systemic stablecoins,
• Systemic issuers to be designated by HM Treasury
and jointly regulated under BoE/FCA oversight
Balance Sheet
Treatment
• Stablecoins treated as reserve-backed digital
payment assets, segregated in bankruptcy-remote

accounts and treated as cash equivalents
• EMTs are fiat-referenced cryptoassets subject to
MiCAR and e-money requirements
• (OCC/FDIC) Mandates prudential capital buffers
tailored to issuer risk profile.
• (Fed) Framework consistent with bank requirements
• EMI issuers: minimum own funds of 2% of EMT
supply; 3% for significant EMTs. Significant issuers
subject to enhanced risk-management requirements
• Systemic: 6 months operating expenses/largest

plausible loss event
• Non-systemic: 1% av. stablecoins in circulation
Capital Reserve
Requirements**

AUTHORS
Marion Laboure is a Managing Director and Macro Strategist at Deutsche Bank in London, and a Lecturer in Economics and Finance at
Harvard University.
She was previously an economist at the IMF, the Central Bank of Luxembourg, Barclays, and the European Commission. She has twice
received the First Prize of the American Society of Actuaries, was named a rising star in finance by Revue Banque, and is recognised

among the 45 standout women in fintech globally.
She holds a Bachelor's degree in Mathematics from the University of Paris Dauphine, a Master of Research in Economics from the
London School of Economics, and a PhD in Econometrics and Quantitative Economics from the École Normale Supérieure.
She is the author of Democratizing Finance published by Harvard University Press.
Camilla Siazon is an Associate on Deutsche Bank’s Macro and Thematic team in London.

She focuses on digital assets, geopolitics, and global macroeconomic trends. Prior to Deutsche Bank, she worked at geopolitical
advisory firms Eurasia Group and Greenmantle.
She holds a dual MA/MSc in International History from Columbia University and the London School of Economics, and a BA in History
from Barnard College, Columbia University.

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 27) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8 
ECB to hike once more  in December, then pause . The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5%  y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer -term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy , and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year . Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets , which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non -manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD  133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though , with  technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key  rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first -home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local -government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a  quarterly pace  until Q2 2027.  Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4% , and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five -year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3%  in Q1 2027 . Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle  in H2 2027. Two government -appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

Global Market Outlook 9

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an  Underweight view on Developed Market ( DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data . With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps  hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3 -7-year part of the  US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yield s to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond -supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda , 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where a ny signal of reduced long -end issuance  
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the f luid geopolitical situation in the Middle East , which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.
